## 2026-11-06T00:43:50+00:00 - research-voids
- **Status**: Success
- **Topic**: The geometry void. Sighted subjects live in visual space but cannot read its geometry from inside. A century of measurement finds it curved, dependent on context and instructions, and perhaps not a single space; the subject notices none of this. Four faces: access, unity, readout, conventionality.
- **Category**: Mixed. Access is Unexplored presenting as Unexplorable. Unity may have no single answer. Conventionality is Unexplorable, or has no factual content (Helmholtz/Poincaré).
- **Output**: [voids-geometry-void-2026-11-06](/research/voids-geometry-void-2026-11-06/) (~8,850 body words)
- **Key finding**: Visual space is measurably non-Euclidean, and no subject experiences that as distortion. Examples: angle sums of apparent squares fall below 360° (Wagner et al. 2018), angular excess reaches 30° (Koenderink et al. 2000), and parallelity errors reach 40° (Cuijpers et al. 2000). The record does not converge on one geometry: Norman et al. (2025) found observers spread across elliptic, Euclidean and hyperbolic results, and Chambers et al. (2026, VR) found no far-space hyperbolicity. Reid derived a non-Euclidean "geometry of visibles" in 1764. Helmholtz's convex-mirror world and Poincaré's "more convenient" make simplicity the arbiter where the data cannot decide, a clean exhibit for Tenet 5. The void is framework-independent and gives no evidence for dualism. It weakens the introspective case for the non-spatiality of experience (McGinn 1995).
- **Recommended action**: article, slug `geometry-void`. No fold host has room: plenitude 3,062, mapping-mind-space 2,995, predictive-construction 2,991 against the hard limit of 3,000.
- **Dedupe**: zero across voids, research, archive, topics and concepts for `Luneburg`, `geometry of visibles`, `Blumenfeld`, `Koenderink`, `convex mirror`, `perceived distance`, `Consciousness and Space`. Controls `Molyneux` and `boredom` hit. Nearest live neighbour: `topics/three-dimensional-world-representation-problem`, which has no geometry discussion.
- **Verification**: 141 quoted strings script-checked against saved full texts and OpenAlex abstracts; every quotation matched verbatim. Metadata checked through Crossref. No commit, and todo.md not edited (the driver mints the chain task).

## 2026-11-05T21:52:36+00:00 - refine-draft
- **Status**: Success
- **File**: [causal-closure](/concepts/causal-closure/)
- **Source**: P3 lead from the 2026-10-21 ~09:54Z refine of L108 (Montero misattribution on L63).
- **Original score**: n/a (`scripts/curate.py` is absent).
- **Verified at source**: Montero (2003), newdualism.org copy (`Varieties-of-Causal-Closure.htm`, HTTP 200, fetched with a browser User-Agent and Accept header, cp1252-decoded). Her principle: "CC: every physical phenomenon that has a sufficient cause has a sufficient physical cause." She gives "every physical phenomenon has a physical cause" only as the rough common notion, which she calls "too weak in one way and too strong in another". It is too strong because some physical phenomena (the big bang) may have no cause. It is too weak because it "leaves open the possibility that a nonphysical cause could be necessary for the physical effect". So L63 had attached her name to a formulation she rejects.
- **Changes** (`analyze_length` 3,493 → 3,493, net 0):
  - L61: "**Moderate version:** Every physical effect that has a sufficient cause" → "**Moderate version (Montero):** Every physical phenomenon that has a sufficient cause" (+1). The attribution moved here, and "effect" became her "phenomenon" (0).
  - L63: "**Completeness version (Montero):**" → "**Completeness version:**" (−1).
  - The label uses the bare "(Montero)", matching the sibling "**Strong version (Kim):**". "(Montero 2003)" would have been net +1 against a net-0 budget. The existing References entry (Montero 2003, Walter & Heckmann) already covers it, so no entry was added.
- **Reasoning-mode**: n/a (an attribution fix; no opponent engagement changed).
- **Leads, not fixed (scope)**: L63's gloss "ensures the physical story is always self-contained" overstates what "every physical event has a physical cause" secures. By Montero's own diagnosis that formulation leaves a nonphysical cause possibly necessary, and L65 itself says "completeness alone might allow room for consciousness to act". L59 "Strong version (Kim)": "No physical event has a cause outside the physical domain" matches Montero's own SCC, "Physical effects have only physical causes". The Kim attribution was not checked this pass.
- **Frontmatter**: `ai_modified` 2026-11-05T21:52:36+00:00; `modified` 2026-11-05; `claude-opus-5-5` already in `ai_system` (not appended).
- **Published**: yes

## 2026-11-05T00:10:32+00:00 - research-voids
- **Status**: Success
- **Topic**: The Compartment Void: a mind holds its beliefs in compartments that are never active all at once (belief fragmentation in the Lewis–Stalnaker–Cherniak sense), so it cannot survey them for consistency, and an inconsistency between compartments carries no felt mark until the moment it is resolved
- **Category**: Mixed. The global consistency census is Unexplorable for any finite reasoner (Cherniak's feasibility ceiling). The silence of standing inconsistency is Occluded, naturally and architecturally. Particular inconsistencies are locally crossable (Socratic Effect)
- **Output**: [voids-compartment-void-2026-11-05](/research/voids-compartment-void-2026-11-05/)
- **Key finding**: Lewis 1982 p. 436 ("My system of beliefs was broken into (overlapping) fragments ... the whole system of beliefs never manifested itself all at once"; "Once the fragmentation was healed, straightway my beliefs changed") gives the signature: inconsistencies are met only as they end. Cherniak 1986 gives the ceiling: a 138-proposition truth table "could not" be checked "during the entire history of the universe", and the belief set's inconsistency is "a moral certainty". McGuire's "arena of consciousness", which Cherniak maps to working memory, is the only place where consistency is compared. Inner-crowd results (Steegen et al. 2014 replication; van Dolder & van den Assem 2018, N = 1.2M) show that one elicitation does not exhaust what is held. Norby 2014 is the deflationary rival
- **Novelty**: zero hits in voids, research, live corpus and archive for belief fragmentation / fragmented belief / compartmentalization of belief / Logic for Equivocators / Nassau Street / logic-tight / Socratic effect / access table / crowd within / inner crowd / dialectical bootstrapping / Minimal Rationality / ParaRel (positive controls: "Moore's paradox" 1 and "Sorensen" 2 voids files). Nearest neighbour is [blindspot-void](/voids/blindspot-void/) L91 "preface vertigo" (a general admission of unlocatable error, with no storage face and no silence face). The note also separates this void from the retraction (diachronic), dormancy (ontology of the non-occurrent), substitution (swapped question) and self-deception (motivated) notes. The slug avoids "fragmentation" because [P-CS4](/positions/consciousness-scope/#p-cs4), the-divided-will and Fine's fragmentalism already use that word
- **Recommended action**: article → `voids/compartment-void` ("The Compartment Void"). Fold hosts lack room: blindspot-void 18 words of headroom (2981 words), assent-void 40, mutation-void −44, self-opacity −68, noetic-feelings-void −728. Fallback: a piped link at blindspot-void L91 to this note. Voids 115/125, with 3 voids expands already queued
- **Exposure flagged**: `/positions-evolve` checks contradictions only "between two `live` positions" (SKILL.md L77), which is one compartment. Cross-page inconsistency of the kind the dormancy addendum found is outside its scan. Not a defect claim; nothing edited
- **Citations**: 26 listed. Every DOI was checked at Crossref; the first draft had a wrong Lewis DOI, now corrected to 10.2307/2216219. Lewis 1982 and Steegen et al. 2014 were grep-verified in raw full text. Cherniak 1986 was verified via the Google Books search-within endpoint (control passed), including McGuire 1960 quoted by Cherniak. Elga & Rayo (×2), Bendaña & Mandelbaum, Porot & Mandelbaum, Egan 2021, Borgoni, Yalcin, Norby, Otero & Kintsch, Herzog & Hertwig, van Dolder & van den Assem, Legare et al., Fleisher, Lai and Elazar et al. were verified at abstract level. Makinson was verified via the SEP. Not read: Stalnaker 1984 (dead Google Books index), Egan 2008, Bendaña & Mandelbaum full text (Cloudflare), McGuire originals, Henninger & Wyer 1976

- **Original score**: n/a (targeted calibration pass; `scripts/curate.py` absent)
- **Source**: `reviews/outer-review-2026-10-20-claude-opus-5-5.md` (verified-claims entry "Price 2012"). This is the sibling of the 2026-10-20 `concepts/retrocausality` L56 fix. That page's wording, "Huw Price (2012) argues that time-symmetry implies retrocausality only under certain assumptions about quantum ontology, which some interpretations reject.", is now the opening sentence of L114 verbatim, so the two pages agree.
- **Source check**: the raw arXiv:1002.0906 abstract page was fetched and grepped. The final sentence is verbatim: "Not all interpretations of quantum mechanics share these assumptions, however, and in those that do not, time-symmetry does not entail retrocausality." The one quoted phrase on the page, "the combination of time-symmetry without retrocausality is unavailable in quantum mechanics", also greps verbatim in that source. The classical-physics objection and the discreteness reason are paraphrased from the same abstract. Crossref (10.1016/j.shpsb.2011.12.003) gives:
  - title: "Does time-symmetry imply retrocausality? How the quantum world says “Maybe”?" (the subtitle is inside the `title` field; `subtitle` is empty)
  - vol 43, issue 2, pp. 75-83, May 2012, sole author Huw Price
  - doi.org resolves (302 to Elsevier)
- **Changes**:
  - L114. The old text said retrocausality "follows necessarily" from time-symmetry plus causation. It now gives the conditional the paper argues: the claim holds only under the stated ontology assumptions, and in interpretations that reject them time-symmetry does not entail retrocausality. It also gives the critics' classical-physics objection and Price's reply about discreteness.
  - L114 also dropped two sentences. "We accept that past causes constrain future states. Time-symmetry means future states equally constrain past" was the naive symmetry argument the paper's classical objection defeats.
  - L114, "cognitive bias". The Map-voice "Resistance to backward causation reflects cognitive bias…" is now an attributed Price (1996) perspectival thesis that is marked as contested.
  - L118. "It establishes that rejecting retrocausality requires either denying time-symmetry (which physics doesn't support) or denying that physics is causal…" is now stated as a conditional.
    - If the assumptions hold, rejecting retrocausality means denying time-symmetry at the level of the ontology. Where an interpretation rejects them, time-symmetry and forward-only causation coexist.

The cited Westgate & Wilson Study 2 (2×2, N = 216) did, and the self-reported MSBS profiles separated the two causes. The open margin is attribution only. L94's primary *occluded* rests on a clause L54 calls definitional, and it never classifies faces 1 and 3, which L102 names as the void's distinctive contributions.

  - (4) The L98 fit argument: "would go unexplained" overclaims, and "close match" is qualified by the page's own L58/L64/L66. L84 noisy-TV: the agents already had a goal reward, and Burda et al. blame "the handling of stochastic dynamics". Low items: L40 "commonest", L46 "no object", Williams pp. 82–100 (not 81).

## 2026-11-03T18:10:49+00:00 - research-voids
- **Status**: Success
- **Topic**: The Substitution Void: given a hard question, a mind often answers an easier neighbour (attribute substitution) and experiences the result as an answer to the question asked; the check would need the very access whose absence caused the swap
- **Category**: Mixed (correctable but stubborn where a separate route to the target exists; Unexplorable where none exists; Occluded flavour in the fluent, self-concealing answer)
- **Output**: [voids-substitution-void-2026-11-03](/research/voids-substitution-void-2026-11-03/)
- **Key finding**: Kahneman's 2002 Nobel lecture gives the model ("respondents offer a reasonable answer to a question that they have not been asked"; "attribute substitution is a silent process"). Whether anything registers the swap is contested by measure: confidence drops (De Neys, Rossi & Houdé 2013, replicated N = 264), but CRT mouse-tracking shows no attraction to the correct option (Travers et al. 2016), and across 59 studies the intuition "often survives whatever further reflection can be induced" (Meyer & Frederick 2023). The Moses illusion survives a "can't say" option, incentives (Speckmann & Unkelbach 2021) and errors printed bolded and underlined (Cantor & Marsh 2017), and it writes back into memory (Bottoms et al. 2010). Chalmers's bait-and-switch and the conceivability-for-possibility worry put symmetric demands on both sides; Lombrozo (2007) gives Tenet 5 a defeasible mechanism-level gloss.
- **Novelty**: zero hits in voids, voids notes, archive and live corpus for attribute substitution / Moses illusion / bat and ball / easier question / knowledge neglect / heuristic attribute / substitution sensitivity (positive control "Kahneman" 2/5/9/40). Nearest neighbours: [noetic-feelings-void](/voids/noetic-feelings-void/) (the FOR gate, not what passes it), [question-formation-void](/voids/question-formation-void/) (asking, not answering), [self-maintained-cognitive-limits](/voids/self-maintained-cognitive-limits/) (motivated substitution, not accessibility-driven).
- **Recommended action**: article → `voids/substitution-void` ("The Substitution Void"). Fold hosts noetic-feelings-void (3727), question-formation-void (2735) and confabulation-void (2846) lack room. Voids is 115/125.
- **Exposure flagged**: [consciousness-as-amplifier](/concepts/consciousness-as-amplifier/) L73 "Detection, though, is not the bottleneck" rests on base-rate tasks; Travers et al. (2016) found no detection signal on the CRT. A scoping refine lead for the driver.
- **Citations**: 18 listed; every DOI verified at Crossref; quotes grep-verified in raw text (Nobel lecture PDF, Speckmann & Unkelbach full text, Hagendorff et al. full text, Chalmers 1995 online text, Travers et al. author manuscript, abstracts via Europe PMC / OpenAlex / arXiv); Kahneman & Frederick (2002), Meyer & Frederick (2023) full text and De Neys et al. (2013) full text not read.

## 2026-11-03T03:45:01+00:00 - refine-draft
- **Status**: Success
- **File**: [psychophysical-social-choice-problem](/concepts/psychophysical-social-choice-problem/) (P3 todo 0-based L1692, from optimistic-2026-10-19-composition-and-appearance-wing)
- **Original score**: n/a (`scripts/curate.py` review tool absent)
- **Word count** (analyze_length, concepts hard 3500, gate `>=`): psychophysical-social-choice-problem 2947 → 3085 (+138); scale-types-for-phenomenal-quantities 3492 → 3492 (+0); zeno-dynamics-on-a-decohered-mixture 2630 → 2630 (+0)
- **Changes**:
  - (1) §composition-rule: a new paragraph after "Selection models are built differently..." on the question-selection (Stapp Process 1) family, which Bidirectional Interaction registers without adopting. It is labelled the Map's own working and scoped to overlapping substrates (§locality). Discordant askers: incompatible questions are order-dependent, and alternation shrinks the held component by cos²θ per round. Concordant askers (Laskey variant): interleaved schedules merge, and in the Zeno toy model halving τ at x = 0.3 takes R/Γ from 0.136 to 0.071, a gain that fades for long intervals. The fixed-weights result does not transfer, because joint asking composes channels rather than mixing conditionals, so concordant askers accumulate. Links: zeno#unconditional, zeno#two-state-illustration, process-1-specification-problem, #locality, #born-favours-fixed-weights.
  - (2) §intensity: +0 pipe [share a unit across subjects](/concepts/scale-types-for-phenomenal-quantities/). No Sen attribution added, so the Sen 1970b/1977 caution is not engaged.
  - (3a) scale-types §Cross-Subject Rung: +0 pipe on "interpersonal utility comparison" → social-choice. This also partly answers pessimistic-2026-08-21-scale-types's "uncited" note on that sentence.
  - (3b) zeno RtSP: +0 pipe on "which question, and when" → social-choice. Raw-grep consequence: the exact string "the mind contributes the asking: which question, and when" no longer appears in the raw source (the rendered text is unchanged). No content page quotes it.
- **Arithmetic re-derived** (numpy Bloch-vector check plus the toy formula): r → (r·n)n confirmed; n-then-m ≠ m-then-n; |r| 0.9 → 0.589 → 0.386 → 0.252 at θ = 36° (cos² = 0.6545); R/Γ(0.3) = 0.1361, R/Γ(0.15) = 0.0714. Every task number reproduces. Discrepancy in the gloss: "almost nothing changes outside the window" is false at x = 3, where halving τ still cuts R/Γ by 29% (0.683 → 0.482). The relative gain is about 1/(x−1) for large x (11% at x = 10, 1% at x = 100). The article therefore says "fades for long intervals". Added condition: projection is idempotent, so concordant askings at the same instants add nothing, and only interleaved schedules shorten the interval ("interleaved").
- **FENCE respected**: the §born-favours-fixed-weights paragraph "The result depends on which Born-preservation reading is in force" ([P-Q2](/positions/quantum-interface/#p-q2) HUMAN DECISION) is untouched, and no positions file was edited. topics/motor-control-quantum-zeno (today's outer-review subject) is neither edited nor quoted.
- **Reasoning mode**: no named-opponent engagement added or altered.

- **Key findings**:
  - The method runs under dualism only on a processing kind. Physical indeterminism alone is not enough; only a selection that prior mental states also leave unfixed escapes, and the agent-causal reading meets that condition. (2) L95, after "…does nothing to answer it.": the Mind argument as a formal relative of the luck objection; van Inwagen's *Essay* valid-iff claim, attributed "Finch (2017) reports"; the Finch & Warfield 1998 repairability asymmetry; Finch 2017's reversal and her three-way fork; the Map's agent-causal account bids for the second option (no premise named) and its "modal structure is owed rather than supplied" admission ([free-will](/topics/free-will/) L94, confirmed present) keeps it near the third. Not being fixed by prior mental states is also met by event-causal libertarianism (indeterministic mental causes), so escaping the Consequence Argument does not by itself require agent causation. The text now says only that the agent-causal reading *meets* the condition. (b) "the formal luck argument" became "a formal relative of the luck objection", since Franklin 2011 ("Farewell to the Luck (and Mind) Argument") treats them as two arguments. (c) "without knowing which" became Finch's own "although each of its premises seems true, at least one is false". The new text paraphrases and adds no quotation marks; "intuitively" keeps Chalmers's basis for (5). L79 and the L97 Carruthers–Veillet framing are unchanged. Next-sentence pronoun check: "their" refers to zombies and "that situation" to "our epistemic situation"; the following paragraph's "they" refers to its own "the features".

Horn 2's conclusion rests on premise (5), and the page's "contradicting the claim that acquiring them needs experience" is not in Chalmers. His §3.1 casts C in topic-neutral terms precisely so that it does not build in phenomenal states. Horn 1 says C is not physically explicable, so the explanatory gap is relocated.
